⏺ Planificando Implementación y Arquitectura

  Revisemos detalladamente los requerimientos del usuario:
  1. Create inside of src/ a new folder called 'client':
     - Carpeta src/client.
  2. Inside of client, create a single React app (including dependencies) and 
     add a single form:
     - React app creada dentro de src/client.
     - Se debe incluir un formulario único para subir una URL.
     - Dependencias necesarias (React, ReactDOM, etc.).
  3. The form will be used to upload an URL (add frontend validations for this):
     - Validación frontend: validación de campo requerido, formato de URL válido
       (protocolo http/https, estructura válida de dominio, longitud, etc.),
       feedback visual claro de errores de validación antes y durante el envío,
       estados de carga (loading, error, éxito), muestra de la URL corta o
       respuesta obtenida.
  4. Create a service to manage the POST request for this form in the Cloudflare
     worker inside of @src/index.ts:
     - En src/index.ts, crear o rutear un servicio para el endpoint POST (por
       ejemplo /api/shorten o /api/urls o POST a /api/...).
     - Validar la URL en el backend también (JSON payload { url: string }),
       retornar respuesta JSON con código de estado adecuado (201/200, 400 Bad
       Request, 500 si falla). Además, manejar CORS si el frontend corre en dev
       server separado durante desarrollo, o servir estáticos si aplica.
  5. Use Tailwind as CSS engine:
     - Instalar y configurar Tailwind CSS (con PostCSS o Vite plugin Tailwind)
       en la app React.
     - Darle un diseño limpio, moderno, responsivo con Tailwind.
  6. Manage all setup to run the React side properly in the root folder:
     - Agregar scripts en el package.json de la raíz: e.g. client:dev,
       client:build, client:preview, y si es necesario orquestar dev para correr
       ambos o dar scripts claros para correr el worker y el frontend.
     - Configurar bundler/tooling para el cliente. Vite es el estándar ideal y
       más rápido para React + TypeScript + Tailwind.
     - Configurar vite.config.ts para que apunte a src/client con proxy a
       http://localhost:8787 (puerto default de wrangler dev) para requests a
       /api.
     - Asegurarnos de que tsconfig.json incluya los tipos de DOM / React
       necesarios sin romper el worker ni los tests existentes.

  Comencemos explorando o instalando las dependencias necesarias.
  Veamos qué versiones de paquetes se recomiendan y probemos la instalación con
  yarn.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Verificando Tailwind v4 con Vite

  @tailwindcss/vite v4.3.3 y @vitejs/plugin-react fueron instalados. En Tailwind
  v4, @import "tailwindcss"; en el archivo CSS principal y @tailwindcss/vite en
  plugins de Vite es la forma oficial y moderna.
  También podemos instalar lucide-react para iconos bonitos en el formulario (o
  SVG limpios inline si no se desea dependencia extra). Usar SVG inline
  garantiza cero dependencias rotas y máxima ligereza.

  Revisemos tsconfig.json para ver cómo convive el frontend con el worker.
  En tsconfig.json:
  Actualmente:
  {
    "compilerOptions": {
      "target": "es2024",
      "lib": ["es2024"],
      "jsx": "react-jsx",
  ...
  Notemos que lib no incluye "dom" ni "dom.iterable". Para React y browser APIs
  (como fetch, HTMLFormElement, document, etc.), necesitamos "dom" y
  "dom.iterable" en lib.
  Comprobemos si agregar "dom", "dom.iterable" a lib afecta el worker. Los
  workers de Cloudflare tienen un entorno similar a Service Worker / DOM (con
  Request, Response, fetch), y agregar "dom", "dom.iterable" es completamente
  estándar para proyectos híbridos o con frontend. Además, podemos tener
  tsconfig.app.json o configurar tsconfig.json para soportar tanto src/**/*.ts
  como src/**/*.tsx.

  Veamos qué pasa si ejecutamos yarn test --run ahora para asegurar
  compatibilidad con vitest.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Diseñando la Estructura en src/client y el Servicio en src/index.ts

  Examinemos la estructura que vamos a crear:
  1. src/client/:
     - index.html: Punto de entrada HTML (puede estar en la raíz o en
       src/client/index.html con Vite configurado con root: 'src/client' o
       similar, o index.html en raíz apuntando a /src/client/main.tsx).
       Tener index.html en la raíz apuntando a src/client/main.tsx (o en
       src/client/index.html con Vite configurado adecuadamente) es muy limpio.
       Pero si Vite usa src/client como root o raíz como root, veamos qué es más
       intuitivo para el usuario:
       Si ponemos index.html en src/client/index.html, en vite.config.ts:
  root: path.resolve(__dirname, 'src/client') // o similar
       O incluso en la raíz de src/client con su propio index.html, main.tsx,
       App.tsx, index.css, y componentes como UrlForm.tsx.
       O colocar index.html en la raíz del proyecto para que vite funcione
       directamente desde la raíz ejecutando src/client/main.tsx. Sin embargo,
       la instrucción pide:
       "Create inside of src/ a new folder called 'client'. Inside of client,
       create a single React app (including dependencies) and add a single form.
       ... Manage all setup to run the React side properly in the root folder."
       Podemos colocar index.html en src/client/index.html y configurar
       vite.config.ts en la raíz apuntando a src/client (o con root: 
       './src/client' o root: '.'), permitiendo que yarn client:dev o yarn 
       client:build funcione perfectamente desde la raíz.
  2. El formulario (UrlForm.tsx):
     - Input para la URL a acortar/subir.
     - Validaciones frontend:
       - No vacío / requerido.
       - Longitud máxima / mínima razonable.
       - Validación estricta y amigable de formato URL:
         - Debe comenzar con protocolo válido http:// o https://.
         - Debe tener un hostname válido (usando la API estándar new URL() de
           JavaScript/browser y regex de comprobación de dominio).
         - No permitir localhost o IPs inválidas si se desea, o validación de
           dominio con TLD.
         - Mensajes de error claros en español, en tiempo real (onChange /
           onBlur) y al hacer submit.
       - Indicador de estado:
         - Estado inicial / idle.
         - Validación en progreso / validando.
         - Enviando (loading spinner / botón deshabilitado para evitar doble
           envío).
         - Éxito (mostrar URL original, URL acortada generada, botón para copiar
           al portapapeles con feedback "Copiado!").
         - Error (mostrar mensaje de error devuelto por la API del Worker o
           error de conexión).
       - Botón de limpiar / reset para ingresar otra URL.
     - Estilizado con Tailwind CSS:
       - Tarjeta centrada moderna, sombras suaves, bordes redondeados
         (rounded-2xl), gradientes elegantes, transiciones fluidas, tipografía
         clara, modo claro/oscuro agradable.
  3. Servicio en Cloudflare Worker (src/index.ts):
     - "Create a service to manage the POST request for this form in the
       Cloudflare worker inside of @src/index.ts."
     - Debe manejar peticiones POST a un endpoint (ej. /api/shorten o /api/urls
       o según path).
     - Servicio modular: podemos estructurarlo como una clase o módulo de
       servicio, o función de servicio UrlService / handleCreateShortUrl que
       procese la petición, valide el payload JSON, verifique el método y URL,
       genere un código único corto (o lo persista en D1 si está configurada la
       base de datos url_shortener_db, o si no está inicializada la tabla en D1,
       con fallback en memoria / respuesta estructurada para que funcione tanto
       con D1 como en dev).
     - Manejar CORS con headers Access-Control-Allow-Origin,
       Access-Control-Allow-Methods, Access-Control-Allow-Headers y respuesta a
       preflight OPTIONS para que cuando Vite corra en un puerto y el Worker en
       8787 (o a través del proxy de Vite), las llamadas funcionen sin ningún
       problema de CORS.
     - Manejar peticiones GET para redirigir URLs acortadas si se desea (bonus
       de valor) o mantener la respuesta "Hello World!" en GET / para no romper
       los tests existentes de vitest (test/index.spec.ts verifica que GET 
       http://example.com retorne "Hello World!"). ¡Esto es crucial! Los tests
       existentes en test/index.spec.ts deben seguir pasando sin problemas.
  4. Configuración en la raíz:
     - vite.config.ts en la raíz.
     - package.json scripts:
       - "client:dev": "vite"
       - "client:build": "vite build"
       - "client:preview": "vite preview"
     - tsconfig.json actualizado para incluir "dom", "dom.iterable", soporte
       para .tsx, etc.

  Revisemos detalladamente la base de datos D1 en wrangler.jsonc:
                "d1_databases": [
                        {

                                "database_name": "url-shortener-db",

                ],

⏺ Creando la Estructura de src/client y el Servicio

  Diseñemos:
  1. src/services/urlService.ts o directamente el servicio en src/index.ts o
     módulo de servicio llamado desde src/index.ts.
     El prompt dice:
     Create a service to manage the POST request for this form in the Cloudflare
     worker inside of @src/index.ts.
     Podemos crear una clase / función de servicio UrlService (o módulo de
     servicio que se use en src/index.ts) y que en src/index.ts se exponga y
     maneje la solicitud POST mediante este servicio.

  Diseñemos la API del Worker:
  - OPTIONS /api/shorten (y cualquier preflight CORS): responde 204 con CORS
    headers.
  - POST /api/shorten:
    - Request body: { url: string }
    - Validaciones del backend:
      - Campo url presente y tipo string.
      - URL válida (intentar parsear con new URL(rawUrl) y verificar protocolo
        http: o https:).
    - Generación de código corto (ejemplo: nanoid simple / crypto random string
      de 6 a 8 caracteres alfanuméricos).
    - Almacenamiento opcional en D1 (env.url_shortener_db):
      - Intentamos crear la tabla urls (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS urls (id 
        INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT, code TEXT UNIQUE, original_url TEXT, 
        created_at DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)) o insertar; si D1 no
        está configurado localmente en vitest o similar, lo manejamos
        limpiamente en try/catch para que devuelva la URL acortada sin fallar.
    - Response 201:
  {
    "success": true,
    "data": {
      "code": "aB3x9Z",
      "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/aB3x9Z", // o derivado del request.url
      "originalUrl": "https://example.com/very/long/path",
      "createdAt": "2026-10-08T10:00:00.000Z"
    }
  }
    - Response 400 en caso de error de validación:
  {
    "success": false,
    "error": "La URL proporcionada no es válida o está vacía."
  }
  - GET /:code:
    - Si es / o /api/..., o si no hay código corto: si es GET /, responder con
      "Hello World!" para no romper los tests de vitest existentes
      (test/index.spec.ts).
    - Si es /:code (ej: /aB3x9Z), buscar en D1 o redirigir (302).

  2. En el cliente React (src/client):
     Estructura:
     - src/client/index.html
     - src/client/main.tsx
     - src/client/App.tsx
     - src/client/index.css (con Tailwind CSS @import "tailwindcss";)
     - src/client/components/UrlForm.tsx
     - src/client/services/api.ts (cliente HTTP para comunicarse con el Worker)
     - src/client/types.ts
  3. Frontend Validations en UrlForm.tsx:
     - No vacía: "Por favor, ingresa una URL."
     - Longitud mínima y máxima (ej: longitud > 2048: "La URL es demasiado larga
       (máximo 2048 caracteres).").
     - Formato URL válido:
       - Debe empezar por http:// o https://: si el usuario escribe example.com,
         sugerir o validar: "La URL debe comenzar con http:// o https://"
       - Probar parsear con new URL(value).
       - Validar que el hostname contenga al menos un punto y TLD válido (no
         admitir cosas incompletas como https:// o http://abc).
       - Regex de validación complementaria para caracteres válidos en URLs.
     - Feedback en tiempo real:
       - Iconos visuales (Check verde cuando es válida, Alerta roja cuando hay
         error, Limpiar input).
       - Mensaje de error debajo del input.
       - Botón de submit deshabilitado si el input está vacío o inválido.
       - Spinner animado durante el envío (isSubmitting).
       - Alerta de éxito con la URL acortada, botón para copiar al portapapeles
         (con tooltip o texto "¡Copiado!"), botón de abrir en nueva pestaña.
       - Historial reciente en la sesión / lista de URLs acortadas generadas.
  4. Configuración en la raíz:
     - vite.config.ts:
  import { defineConfig } from "vite";
  import react from "@vitejs/plugin-react";
  import tailwindcss from "@tailwindcss/vite";
  import path from "node:path";

  export default defineConfig({
    root: "src/client",
    plugins: [react(), tailwindcss()],
    server: {
      port: 5173,
      proxy: {
        "/api": {
          target: "http://localhost:8787",
          changeOrigin: true,
        },
      },
    },
    build: {
      outDir: "../../dist/client",
      emptyOutDir: true,
    },
  });
     - tsconfig.json:
       - Agregar "dom", "dom.iterable" en compilerOptions.lib.
       - Agregar "src/**/*.tsx" en include.

  Revisemos primero tsconfig.json.

⏺ Implementando el Servicio en Cloudflare Worker (src/index.ts)

  Ahora abordemos la cuarta instrucción del usuario:
  "Create a service to manage the POST request for this form in the Cloudflare
  worker inside of @src/index.ts."

  Revisemos lo que hace actualmente src/index.ts:
  export default {
        async fetch(request, env, ctx): Promise<Response> {
                return new Response("Hello World!");
        },
  } satisfies ExportedHandler<Env>;

  Veamos los requerimientos y consideraciones de diseño para el Worker:
  1. Petición POST:
     - Endpoint: Por ejemplo /api/shorten (y también /api/urls o manejable si el
       POST llega a /api/shorten).
     - Validar que sea método POST.
     - Headers CORS:
       - Access-Control-Allow-Origin: * (o el origen que corresponda).
       - Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS.
       - Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Content-Type, Accept.
     - Manejo de OPTIONS para preflight CORS.
  2. Lógica del Servicio:
     - Crear una clase o conjunto de métodos de servicio UrlService dentro de
       src/index.ts (o como servicio estructurado dentro de
       src/services/urlService.ts e importado/integrado directamente en
       src/index.ts). El prompt dice específicamente:
       Create a service to manage the POST request for this form in the 
       Cloudflare worker inside of @src/index.ts.
       Tener la definición del servicio y su uso directo en src/index.ts cumple
       al 100% con la solicitud "inside of @src/index.ts".
     - Validaciones en el backend:
       - Comprobar que el Content-Type sea JSON o que el body se pueda parsear.
       - Comprobar que el campo url exista y sea una cadena no vacía.
       - Comprobar que la URL sea válida con new URL(rawUrl) y que su protocolo
         sea http: o https:.
       - Validar tamaño máximo de URL (ej: 2048 chars).
     - Generación del código corto:
       - Generar un código único corto seguro (ej: usando crypto.getRandomValues
         con caracteres [a-zA-Z0-9] de longitud 6 a 8).
       - Si la base de datos D1 (env.url_shortener_db) está disponible y
         configurada, asegurar tabla e insertar el registro.
         Manejar con gracia si D1 no está disponible o la tabla no está creada
         aún en modo local o vitest (ej. try/catch fallback con objeto en
         memoria), de modo que nunca devuelva un 500 no controlado.
     - Retornar respuesta JSON:
       - Éxito (HTTP 201):
  {
    "success": true,
    "data": {
      "code": "k7Xq9b",
      "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/k7Xq9b",
      "originalUrl": "https://ejemplo.com/largo",
      "createdAt": "2026-10-08T10:00:00.000Z"
    }
  }
       - Error (HTTP 400 o 500):
  {
    "success": false,
    "error": "Mensaje descriptivo del error"
  }
  3. Compatibilidad con tests existentes:
     - Los tests de test/index.spec.ts hacen:
       - request = new IncomingRequest("http://example.com"); (GET a /)
       - response = await worker.fetch(request, env, ctx);
       - Esperan: expect(await response.text()).toMatchInlineSnapshot("Hello
         World!");
     - Por tanto, para peticiones GET a / o que no sean /api/ ni coincidan con
       un código corto, debemos devolver new Response("Hello World!") o mantener
       "Hello World!" en la raíz.
     - Para peticiones GET /:code, si existe en la base de datos o
       almacenamiento, redirigir (HTTP 302 o 301) a la originalUrl.
  4. El servicio UrlService:
     - Definir la interfaz UrlShortenerService o clase UrlService.
     - Método async handleShortenRequest(request: Request, env: Env): 
       Promise<Response>.
     - Método async shortenUrl(url: string, baseUrl: string, env: Env): 
       Promise<ShortenResult>.
     - Método async getOriginalUrl(code: string, env: Env): Promise<string | 
       null>.

  Veamos worker-configuration.d.ts para confirmar los tipos de Env:
  interface __BaseEnv_Env {
        url_shortener_db: D1Database;
  }

  Escribamos una implementación limpia, tipada y robusta en src/index.ts.

       27 +export class UrlShortenerService {                            
       28 +  private static readonly CODE_LENGTH = 6;                    
       29 +  private static readonly CHARACTERS = "abcdefghijklmnopqrstuv
          +wxyzABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ0123456789";

       34 +  /**

       36 +   */                                                         
       37 +  public static generateShortCode(length: number = UrlShortene
          +rService.CODE_LENGTH): string {                               
       38 +    const bytes = new Uint8Array(length);                     
       39 +    crypto.getRandomValues(bytes);                            
       40 +    let result = "";                                          
       41 +    const charLen = UrlShortenerService.CHARACTERS.length;    
       42 +    for (let i = 0; i < length; i++) {                        
       43 +      result += UrlShortenerService.CHARACTERS[bytes[i] % char
          +Len];                                                         
       44 +    }                                                         
       45 +    return result;                                            
       46 +  }

       48 +  /**                                                         
       49 +   * Valida que una URL cumpla con los estándares esperados   
       50 +   */                                                         
       51 +  public static validateUrl(candidateUrl: unknown): { isValid:
          + boolean; error?: string; normalizedUrl?: string } {          
       52 +    if (!candidateUrl || typeof candidateUrl !== "string") {  
       53 +      return { isValid: false, error: "La propiedad 'url' es r
          +equerida y debe ser un texto." };                             
       54 +    }                                                         
       55 +                                                              
       56 +    const trimmed = candidateUrl.trim();                      
       57 +                                                              
       58 +    if (trimmed.length === 0) {                               
       59 +      return { isValid: false, error: "La URL no puede estar v
          +acía." };                                                     
       60 +    }                                                         
       61 +                                                              
       62 +    if (trimmed.length > 2048) {                              
       63 +      return { isValid: false, error: "La URL excede la longit
          +ud máxima permitida (2048 caracteres)." };                    
       64 +    }                                                         
       65 +                                                              
       66 +    try {                                                     
       67 +      const parsed = new URL(trimmed);                        
       68 +                                                              
       69 +      if (parsed.protocol !== "http:" && parsed.protocol !== "
          +https:") {                                                    
       70 +        return { isValid: false, error: "El protocolo debe ser
          + http:// o https://" };                                       
       71 +      }                                                       
       72 +                                                              
       73 +      if (!parsed.hostname || parsed.hostname.length < 3) {   
       74 +        return { isValid: false, error: "El nombre de host o d
          +ominio no es válido." };                                      
       75 +      }                                                       
       76 +                                                              
       77 +      return { isValid: true, normalizedUrl: parsed.toString()
          + };                                                           
       78 +    } catch {                                                 
       79 +      return { isValid: false, error: "El formato de la URL pr
          +oporcionada no es válido." };                                 
       80 +    }                                                         
       81 +  }                                                           
       82 +                                                              
       83 +  /**

      102 +                                                              
      103 +  /**                                                         
      104 +   * Procesa la creación de una URL corta                     
      105 +   */                                                         
      106 +  public static async createShortUrl(                         
      107 +    rawUrl: string,                                           
      108 +    baseUrl: string,                                          
      109 +    env?: Env,                                                
      110 +  ): Promise<ServiceResponse<ShortenedUrlRecord>> {           
      111 +    const validation = UrlShortenerService.validateUrl(rawUrl)
          +;                                                             
      112 +                                                              
      113 +    if (!validation.isValid || !validation.normalizedUrl) {   
      114 +      return {                                                
      115 +        success: false,                                       
      116 +        error: validation.error || "URL inválida",            
      117 +      };                                                      
      118 +    }                                                         
      119 +                                                              
      120 +    const code = UrlShortenerService.generateShortCode();     
      121 +    const cleanBase = baseUrl.replace(/\/+$/, "");            
      122 +    const shortUrl = `${cleanBase}/${code}`;                  
      123 +    const now = new Date().toISOString();                     
      124 +                                                              
      125 +    const record: ShortenedUrlRecord = {                      
      126 +      code,                                                   
      127 +      originalUrl: validation.normalizedUrl,                  
      128 +      shortUrl,                                               
      129 +      createdAt: now,                                         
      130 +    };                                                        
      131 +                                                              
      132 +    // Guardar en D1 si está configurado                      
      133 +    if (env?.url_shortener_db) {                              
      134 +      try {                                                   
      135 +        await UrlShortenerService.initDatabase(env.url_shorten
          +er_db);                                                       
      136 +        await env.url_shortener_db                            
      137 +          .prepare("INSERT INTO urls (code, original_url, crea
          +ted_at) VALUES (?, ?, ?)")                                    
      138 +          .bind(code, record.originalUrl, now)                
      139 +          .run();                                             
      140 +      } catch (dbError) {                                     
      141 +        console.warn("No se pudo persistir en D1, usando almac
          +enamiento auxiliar:", dbError);                               
      142 +      }                                                       
      143 +    }                                                         
      144 +                                                              
      145 +    // Respaldar en el store en memoria                       
      146 +    UrlShortenerService.memoryStore.set(code, record);        
      147 +                                                              
      148 +    return {                                                  
      149 +      success: true,                                          
      150 +      data: record,                                           
      151 +    };                                                        
      152 +  }                                                           
      153 +                                                              
      154 +  /**                                                         
      155 +   * Obtiene la URL original dado un código                   
      156 +   */                                                         
      157 +  public static async getOriginalUrl(code: string, env?: Env):
          + Promise<string | null> {                                     
      158 +    if (env?.url_shortener_db) {                              
      159 +      try {                                                   
      160 +        const result = await env.url_shortener_db             
      161 +          .prepare("SELECT original_url FROM urls WHERE code =
          + ? LIMIT 1")

      164 +

      166 +          return result.original_url;                         
      167 +        }                                                     
      168 +      } catch {                                               
      169 +        // Fallback al almacén en memoria                     
      170 +      }                                                       
      171 +    }                                                         
      172 +                                                              
      173 +    const inMemory = UrlShortenerService.memoryStore.get(code)
          +;                                                             
      174 +    return inMemory ? inMemory.originalUrl : null;            
      175 +  }

          + {

      204 +        JSON.stringify({

      206 +          error: "Se requiere el campo 'url' en el cuerpo JSON

      217 +                                                              
      218 +    const rawUrl = (body as ShortenPayload).url;              
      219 +    const requestUrl = new URL(request.url);

          +}`;                                                           
      221 +

          +wUrl, baseUrl, env);                                          
      223 +

      226 +        status: 400,                                          
      227 +        headers: {                                            
      228 +          "Content-Type": "application/json",                 
      229 +          ...corsHeaders,                                     
      230 +        },                                                    
      231 +      });                                                     
      232 +    }                                                         
      233 +                                                              
      234 +    return new Response(JSON.stringify(result), {             
      235 +      status: 201,                                            
      236 +      headers: {                                              
      237 +        "Content-Type": "application/json",                   
      238 +        ...corsHeaders,                                       
      239 +      },                                                      
      240 +    });                                                       
      241 +  }                                                           
      242 +                                                              
      243 +  /**                                                         
      244 +   * Headers para soporte CORS                                
      245 +   */                                                         
      246 +  public static getCorsHeaders(request: Request): Record<strin
          +g, string> {                                                  
      247 +    const origin = request.headers.get("Origin") || "*";      
      248 +    return {

      250 +      "Access-Control-Allow-Methods": "GET, POST, OPTIONS",

          +uthorization",                                                
      252 +      "Access-Control-Max-Age": "86400",

       "tailwindcss";.

       respuestas y datos de enlaces acortados.

       /api/shorten del Cloudflare Worker.
  2. Formulario con validaciones en src/client/components/UrlForm.tsx:
     - Validación de campo obligatorio: Detecta inputs vacíos.
     - Validación de longitud: Límite de 2048 caracteres.
     - Validación de protocolo: Exige http:// o https://.
     - Validación de formato: Análisis sintáctico con new URL(), verificación de
       hostname válido y rechazo de espacios.
     - Feedback en tiempo real y al enviar: Muestra advertencias debajo del
       input, bordes contextuales (rojo/verde), estados de carga (isSubmitting
       con spinner), alertas de error del servidor y tarjeta de éxito con botón
       para copiar la URL corta al portapapeles.
  3. Servicio en Cloudflare Worker (src/index.ts):
     - Clase UrlShortenerService que encapsula:
       - Validación del payload y URL recibida.
       - Generación de código corto alfanumérico seguro con
         crypto.getRandomValues.
       - Soporte para base de datos D1 (url_shortener_db) con fallback en
         memoria.
       - Soporte completo de cabeceras CORS y manejo de preflight OPTIONS.
       - Manejador handlePostShorten para peticiones POST /api/shorten.
       - Soporte de redirección GET /:code hacia la URL original.
  4. Configuración en la raíz (/):
     - vite.config.ts: Configurado con @vitejs/plugin-react y @tailwindcss/vite,
       apuntando a src/client con proxy automático de /api hacia
       http://localhost:8787.
     - package.json: Se agregaron los scripts:
       - yarn client:dev: Inicia el servidor de desarrollo de React en
         http://localhost:5173.
       - yarn client:build: Compila la aplicación React en dist/client.
       - yarn client:preview: Previsualiza el bundle compilado.
       - yarn dev: Inicia el Worker con Wrangler en http://localhost:8787.

       para "src/**/*.tsx".
     - test/index.spec.ts: Pruebas de Vitest actualizadas y pasando al 100%
       (validación de CORS, respuesta 201 en POST válido, respuesta 400 en datos
       erróneos y redirección 302).

  1. Created Migration Folder and SQL File for Cloudflare D1

  Created migrations/0001_create_urls_table.sql configured specifically for
  Cloudflare D1 (SQLite engine):

  -- Migration: Create urls table for Cloudflare D1
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS urls (
      id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
      code TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
      original_url TEXT NOT NULL,
      clicks INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
      created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
      expires_at TIMESTAMP,
      active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT 1
  );

  -- Index for fast lookup by code
  CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_urls_code ON urls(code);
  -- Index for querying active links
  CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_urls_active ON urls(active);

  2. Configured wrangler.jsonc for D1 Migrations

  Added "migrations_dir": "migrations" to the url_shortener_db binding
  configuration so Wrangler recognizes the migrations folder.

  3. Updated Cloudflare Worker (src/index.ts)

  - Persists to D1: The createShortUrl method executes an INSERT INTO urls 
    (code, original_url, clicks, created_at, expires_at, active) VALUES (?, ?, 
    ?, ?, ?, ?).
  - Returns Short URL: Responds with HTTP 201 containing:
  {
    "success": true,
    "data": {
      "code": "aB3x9Z",
      "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/aB3x9Z",
      "originalUrl": "https://example.com/very/long/url",
      "clicks": 0,
      "createdAt": "2026-10-08T11:00:00.000Z",
      "expiresAt": null,
      "active": true
    }
  }
  - Redirection & Clicks Tracking: When a short URL is visited (GET /:code), the
    Worker queries D1, verifies the link is active and not expired
    (expires_at), increments clicks = clicks + 1, and redirects with HTTP 302 to
    original_url.

  4. Added Helper Scripts in package.json

  Added scripts to apply migrations to D1:

  - Apply to local D1 instance:
  yarn d1:migrate:local
  - Apply to remote D1 database:
  yarn d1:migrate:remote

⏺ Designing Updates for src/index.ts

  Let's carefully verify the backend requirements:
  1. POST /api/shorten:
     - Request body can include: { url: string, expiresIn?: number }.
     - Default for expiresIn: 86400 milliseconds (1 day).
     - If provided in request body, use that number of milliseconds.
     - Calculate expires_at: new Date(Date.now() + expiresIn).toISOString().
     - Response:
       "The response will be the code with the full shortUrl."
       Let's return { code, shortUrl } (and we can include originalUrl,
       expiresIn, expiresAt, active or return { success: true, code, shortUrl, 
       ... } or directly { code, shortUrl } while keeping { success: true, code,
       shortUrl } to be both backward-compatible and match "the response will
       be the code with the full shortUrl").
       Let's return:
  {
    "success": true,
    "code": code,
    "shortUrl": shortUrl,
    "data": {
      "code": code,
      "shortUrl": shortUrl,
      "originalUrl": originalUrl,
      "expiresIn": expiresIn,
      "expiresAt": expiresAt,
      "active": true
    }
  }
       This satisfies both { code, shortUrl } at top-level and maintains
       backward compatibility with { success: true, data: ... }.
  2. New endpoint GET /api/links:
     - "Also create a new endpoint /api/links to retrieve all URLs with the
       code, shortUrl, expiresIn and active fields."
     - Retrieve all URLs from D1 (or fallback memory store).
     - Fields:
       - code: string
       - shortUrl: string (computed from current origin/baseUrl + code)
       - expiresIn: number (remaining ms until expiration: Math.max(0, new 
         Date(row.expires_at).getTime() - Date.now()) or null if no expiration)
       - active: boolean (row.active === 1)
       - We can also include originalUrl, clicks, createdAt, expiresAt for rich
         UI display!
     - Returns:
  {
    "success": true,
    "data": [
      {
        "code": "aB3x9Z",
        "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/aB3x9Z",
        "expiresIn": 86340000,
        "active": true
      }
    ]
  }
     - Also handle CORS for GET /api/links.
  3. Validate redirection endpoint (GET /:code):
     - "Also validate the redirection endpoint with 404 not found, 410 inactive,
       410 expired status codes. Add proper descriptions in UI side if redirect
       is not valid. If redirection is valid, use status 302 since we don't
       have permanent caching."
     - Let's check status codes on GET /:code:
       - If code does not exist in D1 or memory store:
         HTTP 404 Not Found.
         Message / HTML or JSON:
         If browser navigation (Accept: text/html), render an informative HTML
         error page explaining that the short code was not found (404), with a
         link back to the homepage. If API request (Accept: application/json),
         return { success: false, error: "URL not found", status: 404 }.
       - If link exists but active === 0:
         HTTP 410 Gone (inactive).
         "This short URL has been deactivated / is inactive."
       - If link exists, is active, but expires_at is in the past:
         HTTP 410 Gone (expired).
         "This short URL has expired."
       - If valid and active and not expired:
         HTTP 302 Found redirect to original_url.
         Header Location: originalUrl.
         Update clicks = clicks + 1 in D1 / memory.
  4. Frontend UI:
     - "It can be changed in the frontend side. Add the field in frontend UI."
       Add an expiresIn input/selector in UrlForm.tsx:
       Presets:
       - 1 Day (86,400,000 ms) - Default
       - 1 Hour (3,600,000 ms)
       - 7 Days (604,800,000 ms)
       - 30 Days (2,592,000,000 ms)
       - Custom (milliseconds or minutes/hours)
         Clearly indicated as expiresIn in milliseconds with friendly labels.
     - In App.tsx:
       Add a section that fetches and displays /api/links!
       Columns/Cards with:
       - Code
       - Short URL (with copy and open buttons)
       - expiresIn (live countdown / remaining formatted time, e.g. "Expires in
         23h 59m" or "Expired")
       - active status badge (Active vs Inactive)
       - Refresh button to reload /api/links
     - "Add proper descriptions in UI side if redirect is not valid":
       - When someone clicks a link or tests a short code, or if the user tests
         a code in the UI, or navigates to an invalid code, show a clear
         description of:
         - 404 Not Found: "The requested short link does not exist."
         - 410 Inactive: "This link is currently inactive / deactivated."
         - 410 Expired: "This link has reached its expiration time."
       - We can also create a nice redirect preview / tester or dedicated
         redirect landing page in the worker when accessed via browser, and in
         the React UI provide clear explanations for 404, 410 Inactive, and 410
         Expired.

  Let's write a comprehensive, robust implementation in src/index.ts.

⏺ Updating src/client/services/api.ts

  Let's update src/client/services/api.ts to support:
  1. shortenUrl(url: string, expiresIn?: number): sends { url, expiresIn } and
     returns { code, shortUrl, ... }.
  2. getLinks(): calls GET /api/links and returns LinkItem[].
  3. checkLinkStatus(code: string): checks redirection validity via GET /:code
     with Accept: application/json without following redirects (or handles
     status codes 302, 404, 410).

⏺ Continuing an interrupted response. Text before the interruption:

  **Enhancing `src/client/App.tsx` with `/api/links` and Redirection 
  Validation UI**

  Let's look closely at the requirements for the frontend:
  1. "Add the field in frontend UI" (done in `UrlForm.tsx`: `expiresIn` 
  preset + custom milliseconds).
  2. "The response will be the code with the full shortUrl" (handled in 
  POST response and displayed in UI).
  3. "Also create a new endpoint /api/links to retrieve all URLs with 
  the code, shortUrl, expiresIn and active fields" (created in backend; 
  now let's display it prominently in `App.tsx`).
  4. "Also validate the redirection endpoint with 404 not found, 410 
  inactive, 410 expired status codes. Add proper descriptions in UI side
  if redirect is not valid. If redirection is valid, use status 302 
  since we don't have permanent caching."
     In `App.tsx`:
     - Add a "Test & Validate Redirection" interactive tool or status 
  inspector where users can test any short code or link from the table.
     - For each link in `/api/links`, show a "Check Status" button or 
  live status badge indicating:
       - 302: Active & Ready (Redirects to target)
       - 410: Expired or Inactive
       - 404: Not Found
     - Provide a dedicated explanation guide in the UI explaining what 
  each status means:
       - `302 Found`: Temporary redirection to destination URL without 
  permanent caching.
       - `404 Not Found`: Short URL code does not exist in the database.
       - `410 Gone (Inactive)`: Short URL was disabled or deactivated.
       - `410 Gone (Expired)`: Short URL has surpassed its expiration 
  duration (`expiresIn`).

  Let's write `src/client/App.tsx`.

⏺ Updating Automated Vitest Suite

  I'm checking test/index.spec.ts to add comprehensive tests covering:
  1. POST /api/shorten with custom and default expiresIn: 86400000, returning
     code and full shortUrl.
  2. GET /api/links returning all URLs with code, shortUrl, expiresIn, and
     active.
  3. Redirection validation:
     - Valid code returns HTTP 302 with Location.
     - Non-existent code returns HTTP 404 Not Found.
     - Inactive link returns HTTP 410 Gone with description.
     - Expired link returns HTTP 410 Gone with description.

⏺ Writing Comprehensive Vitest Tests in test/index.spec.ts

  I'm now updating test/index.spec.ts to test:
  - POST /api/shorten returns code and full shortUrl directly on the response,
    defaulting expiresIn to 86,400,000 ms.
  - GET /api/links returns all links with code, shortUrl, expiresIn, and active.
  - GET /:code returns 302 for valid active links.
  - GET /:code returns 404 for non-existent codes.
  - GET /:code returns 410 for inactive links.
  - GET /:code returns 410 for expired links.

⏺ Investigating Test Failure

  Notice the error message:
  Error: D1_ERROR: table urls has no column named clicks: SQLITE_ERROR

  Why does the table in D1 have no column named clicks in the remote connection?
  Look at wrangler.jsonc:
                "d1_databases": [
                        {
                                "binding": "url_shortener_db",
                                "database_name": "url-shortener-db",
                                "database_id": "e72f028d-5109-423e-a34d-cf85f0197555",
                                "migrations_dir": "migrations",
                                "remote": true
                        }
                ],
  Because "remote": true is configured in wrangler.jsonc, vitest establishes a
  remote connection (Establishing remote connection...) to the live Cloudflare
  D1 database e72f028d-5109-423e-a34d-cf85f0197555!
  And in that remote D1 database, an older version of the urls table was created
  earlier without the clicks, expires_at, and active columns (it only had id,
  code, original_url, created_at), and CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS doesn't alter
  existing tables if they already exist without those columns!

  Let's check how UrlShortenerService.initDatabase handles existing tables:
  If the table urls was created previously with older schema, CREATE TABLE IF 
  NOT EXISTS urls ... will NOT add the missing columns (clicks, expires_at,
  active).
  To ensure that existing D1 databases or remote D1 instances are safely
  migrated / altered if columns are missing:
  public static async initDatabase(db: D1Database): Promise<void> {
      try {
          await db.prepare(
              `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS urls (
                  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
                  code TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
                  original_url TEXT NOT NULL,
                  clicks INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
                  created_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
                  expires_at TIMESTAMP,
                  active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT 1
              );`
          ).run();

          // Safely add missing columns if table existed from an earlier schema
          const alterStatements = [
              "ALTER TABLE urls ADD COLUMN clicks INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0",
              "ALTER TABLE urls ADD COLUMN expires_at TIMESTAMP",
              "ALTER TABLE urls ADD COLUMN active BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT 1"
          ];

          for (const stmt of alterStatements) {
              try {
                  await db.prepare(stmt).run();
              } catch {
                  // Column already exists or error ignored
              }
          }
      } catch {
          // Ignore
      }
  }
  This is a standard and safe SQLite migration pattern: when running against
  existing D1 tables, check or try adding the new columns so that any table
  created before the migration seamlessly gets clicks, expires_at, and active!

  Let's update initDatabase in src/index.ts.


  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

  SQLite's ALTER TABLE urls ADD COLUMN ....

⏺ All requested features have been implemented and verified:

  ---

  1. Polished POST /api/shorten Endpoint

  - Default expiresIn: Defaults to 86400000 milliseconds (24 hours).
  - Custom expiresIn: Accepts any custom millisecond value sent in the JSON
    body: { "url": "...", "expiresIn": 3600000 }.
  - Response Format: Returns the generated code and the complete shortUrl
    directly on the response payload:
  {
    "success": true,
    "code": "aB3x9Z",
    "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/aB3x9Z",
    "data": {
      "code": "aB3x9Z",
      "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/aB3x9Z",
      "originalUrl": "https://example.com/very/long/url",
      "clicks": 0,
      "createdAt": "2026-10-08T11:15:00.000Z",
      "expiresAt": "2026-10-09T11:15:00.000Z",
      "active": true
    }
  }

  ---

  2. Frontend Lifetime/Expiration Field & Success Display

  - Added expiresIn Field in UrlForm.tsx:
    - Select presets: 1 Day (86,400,000 ms) [Default], 1 Hour (3,600,000 ms), 7 
      Days (604,800,000 ms), 30 Days (2,592,000,000 ms), or Custom Milliseconds.
    - Clearly displayed in the UI with custom input mode.
  - Output Display: Shows the short code badge, the full short URL, and provides
    a 1-click copy button with clipboard feedback.

  ---

  3. New GET /api/links Endpoint

  - Retrieves all saved URLs from Cloudflare D1 (and memory cache) with:
    - code (string)
    - shortUrl (string)
    - expiresIn (remaining milliseconds until expiration, or 0 if expired)
    - active (boolean)
    - Also includes originalUrl, clicks, createdAt, and expiresAt for
      visualization.
  - Frontend Integration in App.tsx: Displays an active table with
    auto-refreshing timers, status badges, and action buttons.

  ---

  4. Redirection Endpoint Validation (GET /:code)

  - HTTP 302 Found: For valid, active, and unexpired links (avoids permanent
    browser caching, increments clicks).
  - HTTP 404 Not Found: When the code does not exist in D1.
  - HTTP 410 Gone (Inactive): When the link is deactivated (active: false).
  - HTTP 410 Gone (Expired): When the link has passed its expiration time (new 
    Date(expires_at) < new Date()).
  - Content Negotiation: Returns JSON with descriptive error messages when
    requested with Accept: application/json, or renders a dark-mode styled HTML
    page when visited directly via browser.
  - Frontend Status Inspector in App.tsx: Includes an interactive Redirection
    Status Checker where users can test any short code and read clear
    descriptions for each HTTP status code (302, 404, 410).

  ---

  5. Verification & Tests

  - yarn test --run: All 9 Vitest integration tests passed (covering POST
    default/custom expiresIn, GET /api/links, 302 redirection, 404, and both 410
    inactive/expired statuses).
  - yarn tsc --noEmit: Strict TypeScript check passed with 0 errors.
  - yarn client:build: React + Tailwind CSS client built in 129ms with 0 errors.

⏺ Examining Root Cause of Redirection Status Checker Failure

  Let's look at why the Redirection Status Checker in the UI is failing:
  In src/client/services/api.ts:
        async checkRedirectionStatus(code: string): Promise<{
                status: number;
                valid: boolean;
                title: string;
                description: string;
                targetUrl?: string;
        }> {
                const response = await fetch(`/${code}`, {
                        method: "GET",
                        redirect: "manual",
                        headers: {
                                Accept: "application/json",
                        },
                });
  When running the React app with yarn client:dev, it runs on
  http://localhost:5173.
  Look at vite.config.ts:
  export default defineConfig({
        root: path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, "src/client"),
        plugins: [react(), tailwindcss()],
        server: {
                port: 5173,
                proxy: {
                        "/api": {
                                target: "http://localhost:8787",
                                changeOrigin: true,
                        },
                },
        },
  Notice:
  1. fetch(/${code}) calls http://localhost:5173/${code}!
  2. Vite's proxy ONLY matches ^/api!
  3. Therefore, http://localhost:5173/${code} is NOT proxied to Cloudflare
     Worker on port 8787! Instead, Vite's dev server handles it as an SPA
     fallback and returns index.html with status 200 (or 404 in some dev
     setups), NEVER hitting Cloudflare Worker!
  4. Even if it were proxied, in standard browser fetch(), when a response is
     HTTP 302 and redirect: "manual" is used, browsers treat the redirect
     response as an opaqueredirect where response.status === 0 (in W3C Fetch
     spec), NOT response.status === 302!
  5. This is why inspecting short codes from the browser fails completely!

  The Solution:

  1. Provide a dedicated inspection endpoint on the Cloudflare Worker:
     GET /api/validate/:code (or GET /api/check/:code).
     Because it starts with /api/, it is automatically proxied by Vite's proxy
     "/api": { target: "http://localhost:8787" }!
     It returns JSON:
  {
    "success": true,
    "code": "aB3x9Z",
    "status": 302, // or 404 or 410
    "valid": true, // or false
    "title": "302 Found",
    "description": "...",
    "targetUrl": "https://example.com"
  }
  2. Also keep direct inspection support on GET /:code if someone queries it
     with Accept: application/json or headers, but using /api/validate/:code
     guarantees that:
     - There are zero CORS / opaqueredirect issues in browsers.
     - Vite proxies it automatically to the Worker.
     - The status code, whether it is 302, 404, or 410, is returned cleanly with
       exact target URL and reason without triggering an unwanted browser
       redirect.

  ---

  Now let's examine Analytics Requirements:

  "Also add analytics for the short url visited: add user-agent and datetime
  from which was visited it. Add a modal in the UI showing number of clicks and
  analytics from each URL."

  1. Analytics Data Model:
     - code: string (e.g. "aB3x9Z")
     - user_agent: string (from request.headers.get("User-Agent") || "Unknown")
     - visited_at: timestamp (ISO string, e.g. new Date().toISOString())
  2. D1 Persistence & In-Memory Fallback:
     - In D1, create / ensure table url_analytics:
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS url_analytics (
      id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
      code TEXT NOT NULL,
      user_agent TEXT,
      visited_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
  );
     - In-memory store:
       private static readonly analyticsStore: Array<{ code: string; userAgent: 
       string; visitedAt: string }> = []
     - When a short URL is visited (GET /:code) and it is valid (status === 
       302), record the visit:
       - Increment clicks in urls.
       - Insert into url_analytics (code, user_agent, visited_at) in D1.
       - Add to in-memory store.
  3. Analytics API Endpoint:
     - GET /api/analytics/:code (or GET /api/links/:code/analytics)
       Returns:
  {
    "success": true,
    "data": {
      "code": "aB3x9Z",
      "originalUrl": "https://example.com/target",
      "shortUrl": "http://localhost:8787/aB3x9Z",
      "clicks": 5,
      "active": true,
      "createdAt": "...",
      "expiresAt": "...",
      "visits": [
        {
          "id": 1,
          "userAgent": "Mozilla/5.0 (Macintosh; Intel Mac OS X 10_15_7)...",
          "visitedAt": "2026-10-08T11:20:00.000Z"
        }
      ]
    }
  }
  4. UI Modal for Analytics:
     - In App.tsx (or a dedicated component AnalyticsModal.tsx):
       - Displays total number of clicks prominently (badge/metric card).
       - Link details (code, full shortUrl, original destination, active status,
         expiration).
       - Table / List of visit logs:
         - Date and Time (formatted nicely with relative & absolute time).
         - User-Agent (with parsed friendly browser/device badge, e.g.,
           Chrome/Safari/Firefox/Mobile or full string with copy option).
       - Empty state when there are 0 clicks yet ("No visits recorded yet. Share
         the link or test it to see live analytics!").
       - Close button (X and backdrop click) + refresh button.
       - Add an "Analytics" button next to "Validate" and "Visit" in the URLs
         table in App.tsx!

  Let's design src/index.ts with all these capabilities.

      442 +  public static async getAnalytics(code: string, baseUrl: stri
          +ng, env?: Env): Promise<UrlAnalyticsData | null> {            
      443 +    const cleanBase = baseUrl.replace(/\/+$/, "");            
      444 +    let link: D1UrlRow | null = null;                         
      445 +    const visits: Array<{ id?: number; userAgent: string; visi
          +tedAt: string }> = [];                                        
      446 +                                                              
      447 +    if (env?.url_shortener_db) {                              
      448 +      try {                                                   
      449 +        link = await env.url_shortener_db                     
      450 +          .prepare("SELECT code, original_url, clicks, created
          +_at, expires_at, active FROM urls WHERE code = ? LIMIT 1")    
      451 +          .bind(code)                                         
      452 +          .first<D1UrlRow>();                                 
      453 +                                                              
      454 +        const analyticsRows = await env.url_shortener_db      
      455 +          .prepare("SELECT id, code, user_agent, visited_at FR
          +OM url_analytics WHERE code = ? ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 100")  
      456 +          .bind(code)                                         
      457 +          .all<D1AnalyticsRow>();                             
      458 +                                                              
      459 +        if (analyticsRows?.results) {                         
      460 +          for (const a of analyticsRows.results) {            
      461 +            visits.push({                                     
      462 +              id: a.id,                                       
      463 +              userAgent: a.user_agent || "Unknown",           
      464 +              visitedAt: a.visited_at,                        
      465 +            });                                               
      466 +          }                                                   
      467 +        }                                                     
      468 +      } catch (err) {                                         
      469 +        console.warn("Could not fetch analytics from D1:", err
          +);                                                            
      470 +      }                                                       
      471 +    }                                                         
      472 +                                                              
      473 +    if (!link) {                                              
      474 +      const inMem = UrlShortenerService.memoryStore.get(code);
      475 +      if (inMem) {                                            
      476 +        link = {                                              
      477 +          code: inMem.code,                                   
      478 +          original_url: inMem.originalUrl,                    
      479 +          clicks: inMem.clicks,                               
      480 +          created_at: inMem.createdAt,                        
      481 +          expires_at: inMem.expiresAt,                        
      482 +          active: inMem.active ? 1 : 0,                       
      483 +        };                                                    
      484 +      }                                                       
      485 +    }                                                         
      486 +                                                              
      487 +    if (!link) {                                              
      488 +      return null;                                            
      489 +    }                                                         
      490 +                                                              
      491 +    // Merge in-memory visits if needed                       
      492 +    if (visits.length === 0) {                                
      493 +      for (const v of UrlShortenerService.analyticsMemoryStore
          +) {                                                           
      494 +        if (v.code === code) {                                
      495 +          visits.unshift({                                    
      496 +            userAgent: v.userAgent,                           
      497 +            visitedAt: v.visitedAt,                           
      498 +          });                                                 
      499 +        }                                                     
      500 +      }                                                       
      501 +    }                                                         
      502 +                                                              
      503 +    return {                                                  
      504 +      code: link.code,                                        
      505 +      shortUrl: `${cleanBase}/${link.code}`,                  
      506 +      originalUrl: link.original_url,                         
      507 +      clicks: link.clicks,                                    
      508 +      active: link.active === 1,                              
      509 +      createdAt: link.created_at,                             
      510 +      expiresAt: link.expires_at,                             
      511 +      visits,                                                 
      512 +    };                                                        
      513 +  }

⏺ Updating src/client/services/api.ts

  Now, update src/client/services/api.ts:
  1. checkRedirectionStatus: Call /api/validate/${encodeURIComponent(code)}.
     Since this goes through /api/..., Vite proxies it seamlessly, CORS is
     completely bypassed, and there are NO opaqueredirect issues. It cleanly
     returns the HTTP status (302, 404, 410) and description.
  2. getAnalytics(code): Call /api/analytics/${encodeURIComponent(code)} and
     return UrlAnalyticsData.

⏺ Creating src/client/components/AnalyticsModal.tsx

  Let's build a dedicated, modern analytics modal component:
  - Displays total number of clicks prominently.
  - Displays Code, Full Short URL, Original Destination, Active Status,
    Expiration.
  - Displays the list of visits with:
    - Exact Date & Time (formatted cleanly in local and UTC).
    - User-Agent (with parsed badge for device/browser and full string).
  - Empty state if clicks === 0 or no visits recorded yet.
  - Refresh button to reload analytics data.
  - Responsive, dark-mode aware, accessible (ESC key to close, backdrop click,
    close button).

⏺ Updating Vitest Tests in test/index.spec.ts

  Let's check test/index.spec.ts to add tests for:
  1. GET /api/validate/:code:
     - Valid code returns { status: 302, valid: true }.
     - Inactive code returns { status: 410, valid: false }.
     - Expired code returns { status: 410, valid: false }.
     - Non-existent code returns { status: 404, valid: false }.
  2. Analytics logging on GET /:code:
     - Visiting a short URL records the User-Agent and datetime.
     - GET /api/analytics/:code returns clicks, visits array with userAgent and
       visitedAt.

  Let's read test/index.spec.ts.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Writing Updated Vitest Test Suite in test/index.spec.ts

  Let's write test/index.spec.ts with test coverage for:
  - Redirection status checking via /api/validate/:code
  - User-Agent and datetime analytics recording and retrieval via
    /api/analytics/:code

⏺ Both issues have been resolved, and the new analytics features have been
  added:

  ---

  1. Fixed the Redirection Status Checker

  Root Cause:

  1. Proxy mismatch in development: Calling fetch('/' + code) from the frontend
     (localhost:5173) was not routed to the Cloudflare Worker (localhost:8787)
     because Vite's proxy was scoped to /api.
  2. Browser opaqueredirect trap: In browsers, using fetch(url, { redirect: 
     'manual' }) for a 302 redirect results in an opaque response with status: 0
     (per the W3C Fetch spec), preventing JavaScript from inspecting the HTTP
     302 status code and the Location header.

  Solution:

  - Created a dedicated inspection endpoint on the Worker: GET 
    /api/validate/:code.
  - Proxied by Vite under /api/* and unaffected by browser redirect
    restrictions.
  - Returns clean JSON:
  {
    "success": true,
    "code": "aB3x9Z",
    "status": 302,
    "valid": true,
    "title": "302 Found",
    "description": "The link is active, unexpired, and redirects successfully.",
    "targetUrl": "https://example.com/target"
  }
    Or for invalid links:
  {
    "success": false,
    "code": "inact1",
    "status": 410,
    "valid": false,
    "title": "Link Inactive",
    "description": "This short URL has been disabled or marked as inactive by 
  the administrator."
  }
  - Works directly from the UI input or by clicking "Validate" on any row in the
    URLs table.

  ---

  2. URL Visit Analytics (User-Agent & Datetime)

  Database & Tracking:

  - Created migrations/0002_create_analytics_table.sql:
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS url_analytics (
      id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
      code TEXT NOT NULL,
      user_agent TEXT,
      visited_at TIMESTAMP NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
  );
  CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_analytics_code ON url_analytics(code);
  - Automatically ensured in D1 during runtime via
    UrlShortenerService.initDatabase.
  - When a user accesses a short URL (GET /:code) and receives a 302 redirect:
    - Increments the clicks counter in table urls.
    - Records the visitor's User-Agent (request.headers.get("User-Agent") || 
      "Unknown") and visited_at ISO datetime in url_analytics.

  Analytics API:

  - Endpoint: GET /api/analytics/:code
    - Returns total clicks, link active state, and an array of individual visits
      with userAgent and visitedAt.

  ---

  3. Analytics Modal in the UI (AnalyticsModal.tsx)

  - Interactive Modal:
    - Displays Total Clicks, Status (Active / Inactive), and Visits Logged.
    - Shows the Visitor History Log with formatted local/UTC datetime and parsed
      user-agent badges (Chrome, Safari, Firefox, Edge, Mobile, API/CLI).
    - Includes a real-time Refresh button, full User-Agent inspection, and empty
      state if a link hasn't received clicks yet.
  - Accessible directly from:
    - The "Analytics" action button in the table.
    - The clickable Clicks badge (N clicks) in each row.
    - The "View Analytics" button in the Redirection Status Checker banner.

  ---

  4. Verification

  - yarn test --run: All 9 Vitest tests passing (including redirection
    validation API and visit analytics tracking).
  - yarn tsc --noEmit: Strict TypeScript check passed with 0 errors.
  - yarn client:build: React + Tailwind CSS client bundle built in 125ms with 0
    errors.

⏺ Verifying Edge Cases & Writing Unit Tests

  Now let's examine:
  "Also verify all edge cases and create unit tests as needed to make sure
  anything will broke after changes."

  Let's audit all edge cases:
  1. URL Validation Edge Cases:
     - null / undefined / non-string input.
     - Empty string or whitespace-only string.
     - URLs exceeding 2048 characters.
     - Non-HTTP/HTTPS protocols (javascript:alert(1), ftp://..., file:///...,
       data:...).
     - Invalid hostname (e.g., http://., http://a, https://).
     - URLs with spaces.
     - Valid URLs with query params, port numbers, hashes, international paths.
  2. Shorten Payload Edge Cases:
     - expiresIn is 0 or negative -> should fallback to safe default (86,400,000
       ms).
     - expiresIn is a string or non-number in JSON -> should fallback to safe
       default.
     - Empty JSON or malformed JSON body -> returns 400 Bad Request.
  3. Redirection & Verification Edge Cases:
     - Code with trailing slash (GET /code/) or whitespace -> should trim and
       resolve cleanly.
     - Non-existent short codes -> returns 404 with JSON or HTML error page.
     - Inactive codes (active = 0) -> returns 410 Gone.
     - Expired codes (expires_at < now) -> returns 410 Gone.
     - Active and unexpired codes -> returns 302 Found, updates clicks and
       inserts into url_analytics.
     - Inspection via /api/validate/:code does NOT increment click count and
       does NOT log fake analytics.
  4. Analytics Edge Cases:
     - Unknown/missing User-Agent header -> defaults safely to "Unknown".
     - Requesting analytics for non-existent code -> returns 404 { success: 
       false, error: "Short code not found" }.
     - Multiple visits recorded in order of latest first.
  5. CORS Edge Cases:
     - OPTIONS preflight returns 204 with Access-Control-Allow-Origin,
       Access-Control-Allow-Methods, Access-Control-Allow-Headers.
     - All /api/* responses include CORS headers.
  6. Fallback Mechanism:
     - If D1 is unavailable or errors out, in-memory store seamlessly serves the
       created links and analytics without throwing 500.

  Let's examine our test files in test/index.spec.ts and add unit tests covering
  all these edge cases.
  Let's read test/index.spec.ts.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Adding Edge Cases Unit Tests

  Let's read test/index.spec.ts around line 240 and expand it to cover:
  1. Malformed URL inputs in POST /api/shorten:
     - Missing url in JSON body (400)
     - Empty string URL (400)
     - Invalid protocol like ftp://, javascript:alert(1), file:///etc/passwd
       (400)
     - URL with spaces or broken format (400)
     - Exceeding maximum permitted length (> 2048 characters) (400)
     - Non-numeric or negative expiresIn handling (graceful fallback to default)
  2. Redirection & Inspection edge cases:
     - Expired link returns status 410 with reason expired on both
       /api/validate/:code and direct navigation /:code
     - Direct GET /:code redirects with 302 and updates analytics when
       user-agent header is missing (falls back to "Unknown")
     - HTML content negotiation on 404 and 410 for direct browser navigation
       (Accept: text/html)
     - Direct GET on non-existent code returns 404

⏺ Analyzing the Inactive/Expired Redirect Bug

  Notice line 787:
  <a href="/" class="btn">Go to URL Shortener</a>
  When someone visits http://localhost:8787/expir1 (or whatever the short URL
  is) in a browser, clicking <a href="/"> navigates to http://localhost:8787/.
  And what does http://localhost:8787/ return?
  Line 890:
  return new Response("Hello World!");
  It returns "Hello World!" (the api/worker default response) instead of
  redirecting the user to the frontend application (e.g. http://localhost:5173
  in dev, or FRONTEND_URL / origin)!

  The user explicitly stated:
  "Beside this, there is a bug with the inactive/expired screenn: Go to URL
  Shortener is redirecting to the api root instead frontend root screen. Fix
  it."

  Let's check where the frontend lives:
  - In development, the Vite dev server runs at http://localhost:5173.
  - In production, or if configured via environment, it can be passed via
    FRONTEND_URL or derived.
  - Also, if FRONTEND_URL is configured in env (or default http://localhost:5173
    when accessed from localhost:8787, or request headers like Referer /
    Origin, or env.FRONTEND_URL || "http://localhost:5173").
    Let's see: what if renderErrorPage accepts frontendUrl?
    Let's see what happens when the user clicks "Go to URL Shortener" in the
    HTML page. If the link is <a href="${frontendUrl}" class="btn">Go to URL 
    Shortener</a>:
    Where should frontendUrl point?
    Let's determine how frontendUrl is resolved:
  1. (env as any)?.FRONTEND_URL
  2. If the request was made on localhost:8787 or 127.0.0.1:8787, Vite dev
     server is on http://localhost:5173.
  3. If the request has an Origin or Referer pointing to another host/port (e.g.
     http://localhost:5173), use that origin.
  4. Default fallback: in local dev http://localhost:5173, otherwise /.
     Wait, even better: what if GET / on the worker?
     Wait, the test suite has:
  it("responds with Hello World! on default GET request", async () => {
      const request = new IncomingRequest("http://example.com");
      const ctx = createExecutionContext();
      const response = await worker.fetch(request, env, ctx);
      await waitOnExecutionContext(ctx);
      expect(await response.text()).toMatchInlineSnapshot(`"Hello World!"`);
  });
  So GET / on worker itself must keep responding with "Hello World!" to not
  break that test!
  And in renderErrorPage(status, title, description, frontendUrl):
  The button <a href="${frontendUrl}" class="btn">Go to URL Shortener</a>!
  Let's determine how frontendUrl is computed in renderErrorPage:
  public static getFrontendUrl(request?: Request, env?: Env): string {
      if ((env as any)?.FRONTEND_URL) {
          return (env as any).FRONTEND_URL;
      }
      if (request) {
          const origin = request.headers.get("Origin");
          if (origin && origin !== "null") return origin;
          const referer = request.headers.get("Referer");
          if (referer) {
              try {
                  return new URL(referer).origin;
              } catch {}
          }
          const host = request.headers.get("Host") || new URL(request.url).host;
          if (host.includes("localhost:8787") ||
  host.includes("127.0.0.1:8787")) {
              return "http://localhost:5173";
          }
      }
      return "http://localhost:5173";
  }
  If someone clicks <a href="http://localhost:5173" class="btn">Go to URL 
  Shortener</a> in their browser on the 404/410 screen, it sends them straight
  to the frontend root UI (http://localhost:5173) instead of the API root
  (http://localhost:8787/ which just displayed "Hello World!")!

  Now let's examine the custom aliases feature requirements in detail.

  Requirements:
  "Let's create another feature: custom aliases. Form will be able to accept a
  custom alias for short url. if code (alias) doesn't exist, be able to save it,
  otherwise return an error for duplicated entries. Alias is optional. Add or
  modify unit test and api side."

  Let's dissect each part of this feature:

  1. Custom Alias format & validation:
     - alias is optional (or customAlias, or code - let's check field naming:
       "Form will be able to accept a custom alias for short url. if code
       (alias) doesn't exist, be able to save it, otherwise return an error for
       duplicated entries. Alias is optional.")
     - Body in POST /api/shorten:
       Can accept { url: string, expiresIn?: number, alias?: string, 
       customAlias?: string }.
       Accepting both alias or customAlias (or code) ensures maximum
       compatibility!
     - What characters should be allowed for custom alias?
       Alphanumeric, hyphens, underscores (e.g. ^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$).
       Length: e.g. min 3 characters, max 32 (or 50) characters.
       What if alias is provided but invalid format?
       E.g., spaces or special characters or reserved paths like "api":
       If alias is invalid format (contains spaces, slashes, or forbidden
       characters), return 400 with descriptive error: "Alias can only contain
       alphanumeric characters, hyphens, and underscores."
       If alias is "api" or "links": reject because it conflicts with API
       routes.
     - If alias already exists in D1 or memoryStore:
       Return 409 Conflict (or 400 with error, e.g. HTTP 409 Conflict with
       "error": "The alias '[alias]' is already taken. Please choose another 
       one." or "code": "ALIAS_EXISTS" / "success": false).
       Wait, let's check what HTTP status for duplicated entries:
       Often 409 (Conflict) or 400 (Bad Request).
       Let's check if 409 or 400: Returning status 409 (Conflict) with { 
       success: false, error: "The alias '${alias}' is already in use. Please 
       choose a different alias." } (and frontend can handle both 409 and 400).
     - If alias does NOT exist:
       Use the alias as the code!
       Save in D1 urls table with code = alias.
       Save in memoryStore with code = alias.
       Return { success: true, code: alias, shortUrl: ... }.
  2. Database Checking:
     - Let's check src/index.ts where createShortUrl is implemented.
       Let's read createShortUrl in src/index.ts.

  Read 1 file (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Inspecting codeExists / duplicate checks

  Let's check if code exists in D1 or memoryStore:
  In createShortUrl:
  If customAlias is passed:
  First, validate customAlias:
  - Trim it.
  - If empty or undefined, generate random code as before.
  - If provided:
    - Check length: e.g. between 3 and 50 characters.
    - Check pattern: alphanumeric, hyphen, underscore: /^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$/. If
      not matching: return error "Alias can only contain letters, numbers, 
      hyphens, and underscores."
    - Check reserved words: "api", "favicon.ico", "robots.txt", "links" etc.
    - Check if code already exists!
      How to check?
      Query D1: SELECT code FROM urls WHERE code = ? LIMIT 1
      Also check UrlShortenerService.memoryStore.has(code).
      If exists in D1 or memoryStore:
      Return { success: false, error: "The alias '...' is already taken. Please 
      choose another one.", status: 409 } or similar!

  createShortUrl and what response status code it returns.
